<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the existential theory of the reals lies in the counting hierarchy. More generally, we show that the truth of existential–universal sentences over the reals can be decided in a fixed level of the counting hierarchy, even when the integer polynomials are given by arithmetic circuits.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an absolute integer $j$ such that $$\exists\forall\mathbb R_{\mathrm{circ}}\in\mathsf C_{j}\mathsf P.$$ The level $j$ is independent of the input length, the numbers of variables, the degrees of the circuit polynomials, and their coefficient magnitudes.
  - Explanation: The circuit-encoded existential–universal real language belongs to a uniform fixed counting level, independent of dimension, degree and coefficient magnitude.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-counting)): Fix $a\ge0$. Let $b(w)$ and $s(w)$ be polynomially bounded nonnegative integers computable in polynomial time. If $A(w,i)$, for $i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}$, is a predicate computable in $\mathsf F_{a}$, then $$C(w)=\#\{i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}:A(w,i)\}$$ is in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$. Existential and universal quantification over these indices are also in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$.* *If $f(w,i)$ is in $\mathsf F_{a}$ and $0\le f(w,i)<2^{s(w)}$, then $\sum_i f(w,i)$ is in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$. The same conclusion holds for sums modulo a positive short integer.
  - Explanation: Counting, short-index quantification and bounded summation preserve availability with the stated increase in function level.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-field-coefficient)): Let $V_w(x,t)$ be a controlled family, where $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_h)$ is a selected subset of its indeterminates and $t$ denotes the remaining ones. Let $\beta\in\mathbb N^h$ be a short index. Then $[x^\beta]V_w(x,t)$ is a controlled family, uniformly in $\beta$. If the evaluation of $V$ is in $\mathsf F_{a}$, the coefficient evaluations are in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$, with cutoff $\max(\lambda,d+1)$, degree bound $d$, and coefficient-norm bound $2^b$.
  - Explanation: Finite-field coefficient extraction preserves control with bounds and cutoff determined by the primary family data.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-controlled-closure)): Controlled families are preserved by each of the following operations, applied a fixed number of times:* 1. *sums and products over short index sets, with uniform bounds on the summands or factors;* 2. *powers with short nonnegative exponents computable in polynomial time, and substitution of controlled polynomials for indeterminates;* 3. *extraction of indexed coefficients in any subset of the indeterminates, and differentiation in one variable to a short indexed nonnegative order.* *In the first operation the index domain is specified in polynomial time; more generally, an available predicate $A(w,i)$ may restrict the indices when polynomial-time uniform bounds for the unrestricted domain are retained. This predicate depends only on the primary word and the family index, not on an evaluation prime or evaluation point. All resulting bounds and prime cutoffs are computable in polynomial bit time from the primary family data. In particular, they are independent of evaluation primes.
  - Explanation: Controlled families survive fixed nesting of sums, products, powers, substitutions, indexed coefficients and derivatives; the nesting restriction is essential.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-crt-sign)): Let $X(w)\in\mathbb Z$ and suppose that nonnegative short integers $m(w)$ and $\lambda(w)$ are computable in polynomial time, with $\|X(w)\|\le2^{m(w)}$. Define $$\label{eq:crt-bounds} Z=\max(16,m,\lambda),\qquad Y=2Z^4.$$ If $X(w)\bmod p$, represented in $\{0,\ldots,p-1\}$, is uniformly computable in $\mathsf F_{a}$ for primes $\lambda<p\le Y$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}X(w)$ is in $\mathsf F_{\max(a,5)+3}$.
  - Explanation: Bounded integer signs are recovered from prime residues using a dyadic circular approximation and the first flagged doubling index.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-controlled-sign)): If a controlled family has no indeterminates, its sign is available, uniformly in any short indices belonging to its input word.
  - Explanation: An indeterminate-free controlled family supplies a uniform available sign decision.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monomial-basis)): The functional $\mathcal L$ vanishes on $(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ and hence descends to $\mathcal A$. If $\deg_x v\le n(\ell-1)$, then $$\label{eq:low-degree-functional} \mathcal L(v)=[x^\tau]v.$$ The algebra $\mathcal A$ is a free $S$-module with basis $\{x^a:0\le a_i<\ell\}$.
  - Explanation: The formal coefficient functional descends to the quotient and proves that the bounded-exponent monomials form a free basis.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-trace)): For the algebra in Equation <a href="#eq:quotient-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quotient-algebra">[eq:quotient-algebra]</a>, over every commutative ring $S$ with identity, $$\label{eq:trace-formula} \operatorname{tr}(M_v)=\mathcal L(vJ) \qquad(v\in S[x]).$$
  - Explanation: Multiplication traces equal the formal coefficient functional applied to the objective times the Jacobian, over arbitrary commutative coefficient rings.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-real-eigenline)): Suppose that the coefficients of the relations in Equation <a href="#eq:quotient-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quotient-algebra">[eq:quotient-algebra]</a> and of $v$ lie in $\mathbb C$, and write $Q(y)=\det(yI-M_v)$. Every common zero $\xi\in\mathbb C^n$ of the $f_i$ satisfies $Q(v(\xi))=0$. If all coefficients are real and $\eta$ is a simple real root of $Q$, there is a common zero $\xi\in\mathbb R^n$ with $v(\xi)=\eta$. Moreover, for any polynomial $g$ over $\mathbb C$, $$\label{eq:composition-determinant} M_{g(v)}=g(M_v),\qquad \det M_{g(v)}=\prod_{Q(\alpha)=0}g(\alpha),$$ where the roots in the product are counted with multiplicity.
  - Explanation: Common zeros yield multiplication eigenvalues; a simple real characteristic root is realized at a real common zero. Polynomial composition gives the determinant product identity.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-char-coeff)): Consider fixed families $r_1,\ldots,r_n,v\in\mathbb Z[x]$, indexed by a word of length $N$. Suppose that $n\ge1$ is polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, that $\ell\ge2$ and $\Omega\ge0$ are short polynomial-time computable integers, and that $\deg_x r_i<\ell$ and $\deg_x v\le\Omega$. Put $$f_i=x_i^\ell-r_i,\qquad J=\det(\partial_jf_i),\qquad D=\ell^n,\qquad L_0=D\Omega+n(\ell-1).$$ For $a\ge0$, suppose that $v$, the indexed $r_i$, and $J$ can be evaluated uniformly in $\mathsf F_{a}$ over prime fields with $$\label{eq:char-prime-bound} p>n(\ell-1)+\ell L_0+D^2+10.$$ Additional short, polynomial-time computable lower cutoffs on $p$ are allowed. Then, for every $0\le d\le D$, $$[y^d]\det(yI-M_v)\pmod p$$ is uniformly computable in $\mathsf F_{a+8}$ in this prime range. All running-time and query-length bounds are polynomial in the length of the input, including $p$ and the coefficient index $d$.
  - Explanation: Trace residues and a truncated exponential recover indexed characteristic coefficients over primes satisfying the stated cutoff.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant)): Let $n\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\ell\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. Suppose that $r_1,\ldots,r_n,v$ are controlled integer polynomial families in variables $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ and a polynomially bounded number of parameter variables $t$, uniformly in $i$, and that $\deg_x r_i<\ell$. In the monomial basis of $$\mathbb Z[t,x]/(x_1^\ell-r_1,\ldots,x_n^\ell-r_n),$$ let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$. Then $$\label{eq:controlled-characteristic} Q(t,y)=\det(yI-M_v)$$ is a controlled family, monic of $y$-degree $D=\ell^n$. If $d$ and $2^b$ are common total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds on the $r_i,v$, and $T=d+n(\ell-1)$, valid total-degree and log-norm bounds on $Q$ are $$\label{eq:determinant-size-bounds} d_Q=D\bigl(1+d(1+T)\bigr),\qquad b_Q=D^2+D\bigl(1+b(1+T)\bigr).$$ The construction is compatible with specializing any parameters.
  - Explanation: The multiplication characteristic polynomial is controlled with explicit total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds, compatibly with parameter specialization.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-root-labels)): Let $P(t)$ and $b_0(t)$ be controlled families of integer univariate polynomials, indexed by the same input word. There is a uniformly controlled family $L_a(t)\in\mathbb Z[t]$, with labels $a$ of polynomial binary length in a polynomial-time specified range, such that whenever $P\ne0$:* 1. *For every label $a$ and every real root $\alpha$ of $P$, either $L_a(\alpha)\ge\tfrac12$ or $L_a(\alpha)\le-\tfrac12$.* 2. *For every label $a$ and every real root $\alpha$ of $P$, if $L_a(\alpha)>0$, then $b_0(\alpha)>0$.* 3. *For every real root $\alpha$ of $P$ with $b_0(\alpha)>0$, some label $a$ satisfies $L_a(\alpha)>0$ and $L_a(\beta)<0$ at every distinct real root $\beta$ of $P$.* *The construction is defined and controlled also when $P=0$, but no root-selection property is asserted in that case. It uses a fixed number of applications of the controlled-family rules, independent of the degrees. Taking $b_0=1$ permits every real root to be selected.
  - Explanation: Short discrete labels can select any prescribed real root satisfying an auxiliary positivity test, including roots with multiplicity. The zero polynomial has no selection guarantee.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-root-value-bounds)): Let $P\in\mathbb Z[t]$ be nonzero, with $\deg P\le d$ and $\left\lVert P\right\rVert_1\le2^b$, where $d\ge2$ and $b\ge1$ are integers. If $g\in\mathbb Z[t]$ satisfies $\deg g\le d$ and $\left\lVert g\right\rVert_1\le2^{b+d^2}$, put $$B_0=b+d^2+d(b+1),\qquad B=d(bd+B_0)+bd+2.$$ At every complex root $\alpha$ of $P$, either $g(\alpha)=0$ or $$2^{-B}\le \|g(\alpha)\|\le2^B.$$
  - Explanation: Nonzero test values at complex roots are bounded above and away from zero without assuming monicity or squarefreeness.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-thom-separation)): For a nonzero real polynomial $P$ of degree at most $d$, distinct real roots have distinct strings $$\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}P^{(j)}(\alpha)\bigr)_{1\le j\le d}.$$
  - Explanation: Derivative-sign strings distinguish distinct real roots, including repeated roots.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-sign)): For integers $B\ge1$ and $s\ge1$, define $$\begin{aligned} C_{B,s}(z) &=\prod_{k=-B}^{B} \bigl(2^{\max(0,k)}+2^{\max(0,-k)}z\bigr)^{2s},\\ A_{B,s}(z) &=\frac{C_{B,s}(z)-C_{B,s}(-z)} {C_{B,s}(z)+C_{B,s}(-z)}. \end{aligned}$$ The denominator is at least $1$ for every real $z$, and $A_{B,s}(0)=0$. For $2^{-B}\le\|z\|\le2^B$, $$\|A_{B,s}(z)-\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}z\|\le2\cdot3^{-2s}.$$ Moreover $C_{B,s}$ is an integer polynomial with $$\deg C_{B,s}=2s(2B+1),\qquad \left\lVert C_{B,s}\right\rVert_1\le2^{2s(2B+1)(B+1)}.$$
  - Explanation: An integer product gives a rational sign approximation with a positive denominator and explicit degree, norm and error bounds.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-controlled-existential)): Suppose that an input word specifies a conjunction of polynomially many equations and strict inequalities $$A_i(x)=0,\qquad B_j(x)>0,$$ in polynomially many real variables. Assume that the numbers of variables and tests, and the type of each addressed test, are computable in polynomial time, and that the polynomials form controlled families uniformly in their indices. Then the existence of a real solution is an available decision. Its counting level depends only on the fixed family constructions and their evaluation algorithms, not on the input or any short outer indices.
  - Explanation: Real feasibility for uniformly controlled equations and strict inequalities is available at a level depending on the fixed constructions rather than the input.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-minimum)): For the nonnegative polynomial $F$ and deformation in Equation <a href="#eq:existential-deformation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:existential-deformation">[eq:existential-deformation]</a>, the minimum $$m(u)=\min_{x\in\mathbb R^n}h_u(x)$$ exists for every real $u\ge0$. The function $m$ is continuous and nondecreasing, and $Q(u,m(u))=0$. Furthermore, $$F\text{ has a real zero} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad m(u)\text{ remains bounded as }u\to+\infty.$$
  - Explanation: The coercive deformation has a continuous nondecreasing minimum satisfying the critical-value polynomial; boundedness is equivalent to a real zero.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-existential-discriminant)): The polynomial $Q(0,y)$ has $D$ distinct complex roots. If $\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_1\le2^H$, where $H\ge0$ is an integer, there is a nonzero polynomial $\Delta\in\mathbb Z[u]$ such that every parameter where $Q(u,y)$ has a repeated complex root is a zero of $\Delta$, and $$\label{eq:existential-discriminant-bound} \left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_1\le2^{H_\Delta},\qquad H_\Delta=(2D)^2+2D(H+D+1).$$
  - Explanation: Separated unperturbed critical values make the exceptional-parameter determinant nonzero and bound its coefficient norm.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-specialization)): Let $F\in\mathbb Z[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ be nonnegative on $\mathbb R^n$, let $n\ge1$, and choose an odd $\ell\ge3$ with $\ell>\deg F$. Define $G,h_u,f_i,Q,D$ by Equations <a href="#eq:existential-deformation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:existential-deformation">[eq:existential-deformation]</a> and <a href="#eq:existential-characteristic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:existential-characteristic">[eq:existential-characteristic]</a>. Let $H,V$ be nonnegative integers bounding $\log_2\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_1$ and $\deg_uQ$, respectively, and choose the parameters in Equation <a href="#eq:existential-specialization-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:existential-specialization-parameters">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>. Then $$q(y)=Q(U,y)$$ is monic and squarefree, has degree $D$ and coefficient norm at most $2^K$, and satisfies $$\label{eq:existential-root-equivalence} F\text{ has a real zero} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q\text{ has a real root less than }R.$$ Every real root of $q$ is the value of $h_U$ at a real common zero of $f_1(\cdot,U),\ldots,f_n(\cdot,U)$.* *When $F$ varies in a controlled family, $\ell$ is chosen from its degree bound as above, and $H,V$ are polynomial-time computable short bounds, $q$ is controlled. All remaining bound parameters in Equation <a href="#eq:existential-specialization-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:existential-specialization-parameters">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>, including $D,H_\Delta$, are computable in polynomial time and have polynomial binary length.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large explicit specialization is squarefree and converts real-zero existence into a real characteristic root below the cutoff.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quartic-fibers)): From a well-formed input of length $N$, one can construct in polynomial time an explicitly listed polynomial $F\in\mathbb Z[x_1,\ldots,x_r,z_1,\ldots,z_n]$, with $n\ge1$ polynomially bounded in $N$, such that $F\ge0$ on real inputs, $\deg F\le4$, and, for every $x\in\mathbb R^r$, $$\label{eq:fiber-encoding} \exists y\in\mathbb R^s\;\neg\Phi(x,y) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \exists z\in\mathbb R^n\;F(x,z)=0.$$ The monomial count and coefficient bit lengths of $F$ are polynomially bounded in $N$.
  - Explanation: Arithmetic and Boolean wire constraints give an explicitly listed nonnegative quartic encoding counterexamples separately in every parameter fiber.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-degree-pieces)): For $0\le d\le d_E$, let $$A_d=\{x\in\mathbb R^r:E_d(x)\ne0,\ E_j(x)=0\text{ for }j>d\}.$$ The sets $A_d$ partition $\mathbb R^r$. Within each $A_d$, the good parameters form a subset that is both open and closed in the relative topology.
  - Explanation: Goodness is locally constant on each actual-degree piece of the growth-test polynomial.
- **C22** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-candidate-coordinates)): Let $W\in\mathbb R[c_1,\ldots,c_k]$ be nonnegative on $\mathbb R^k$, where $k\ge1$, and let $C$ be a nonempty subset of $Z=\{W=0\}$ that is both open and closed in $Z$. Choose an odd integer $\ell\ge3$ with $\ell>\deg W$, and put $$G(c)=\sum_{i=1}^k c_i^{\ell+1},\qquad H_w(c)=G(c)+(\ell+1)wW(c).$$ In the quotient by $$\label{eq:candidate-relations} c_i^\ell+w\frac{\partial W}{\partial c_i}=0 \qquad(1\le i\le k),$$ form the coordinate multiplication polynomials $$\label{eq:coordinate-polynomials} P_i(w,T)=\det(TI-M_{c_i})=\sum_j P_{i,j}(T)w^j.$$ For each $i$, let $j_i$ be the largest index for which $P_{i,j_i}\ne0$. There exists $c^*\in C$ such that $$P_{i,j_i}(c_i^*)=0\qquad(1\le i\le k).$$ If $W$ is a controlled family of integer polynomials, $k$ is polynomially bounded, and $\ell$ is short and computable in polynomial time, then the polynomials $P_i$ and $P_{i,j}$ are uniformly controlled.
  - Explanation: Penalty minimizers converge into a selected relatively open and closed zero-set part; highest parameter coefficients provide coordinate-root candidates.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-etr-bound)): For the circuit encoding of the existential theory of the reals used in this paper, $$\mathsf{ETR}\in\mathsf C_{26}\mathsf P, \qquad \exists\mathbb R\subseteq\mathsf C_{26}\mathsf P.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit level accounting places circuit-encoded existential real feasibility and its many-one reduction class in the stated counting level.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-selection)): Let $p\in\mathbb R[y]$ be a nonzero polynomial of degree $d\ge1$, and let $S$ be a nonempty set of distinct real roots of $p$. There is a list of at most $\lceil\log_2\|S\|\rceil$ conditions $$\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}p^{(j)}(\alpha)=v, \qquad 1\le j\le d,\quad v\in\{-1,0,1\},$$ satisfied by exactly one member of $S$.
  - Explanation: Repeatedly retaining a smallest derivative-sign class selects a root with logarithmically many conditions; a singleton needs the empty list.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-trace",
        "exact_quote": "For the algebra in Equation <a href=\"#eq:quotient-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient-algebra\">[eq:quotient-algebra]</a>, over every commutative ring $S$ with identity, $$\\label{eq:trace-formula}\n \\operatorname{tr}(M_v)=\\mathcal L(vJ)\n \\qquad(v\\in S[x]).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-trace",
          "exact_quote": "For the algebra in Equation <a href=\"#eq:quotient-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient-algebra\">[eq:quotient-algebra]</a>, over every commutative ring $S$ with identity, $$\\label{eq:trace-formula}\n \\operatorname{tr}(M_v)=\\mathcal L(vJ)\n \\qquad(v\\in S[x]).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-real-eigenline",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the coefficients of the relations in Equation <a href=\"#eq:quotient-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient-algebra\">[eq:quotient-algebra]</a> and of $v$ lie in $\\mathbb C$, and write $Q(y)=\\det(yI-M_v)$. Every common zero $\\xi\\in\\mathbb C^n$ of the $f_i$ satisfies $Q(v(\\xi))=0$. If all coefficients are real and $\\eta$ is a simple real root of $Q$, there is a common zero $\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^n$ with $v(\\xi)=\\eta$. Moreover, for any polynomial $g$ over $\\mathbb C$, $$\\label{eq:composition-determinant}\n M_{g(v)}=g(M_v),\\qquad\n \\det M_{g(v)}=\\prod_{Q(\\alpha)=0}g(\\alpha),$$ where the roots in the product are counted with multiplicity.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },

Every common zero $\\xi\\in\\mathbb C^n$ of the $f_i$ satisfies $Q(v(\\xi))=0$. If all coefficients are real and $\\eta$ is a simple real root of $Q$, there is a common zero $\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^n$ with $v(\\xi)=\\eta$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-char-coeff",
        "exact_quote": "Consider fixed families $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v\\in\\mathbb Z[x]$, indexed by a word of length $N$. Suppose that $n\\ge1$ is polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, that $\\ell\\ge2$ and $\\Omega\\ge0$ are short polynomial-time computable integers, and that $\\deg_x r_i<\\ell$ and $\\deg_x v\\le\\Omega$. Put $$f_i=x_i^\\ell-r_i,\\qquad\n J=\\det(\\partial_jf_i),\\qquad\n D=\\ell^n,\\qquad L_0=D\\Omega+n(\\ell-1).$$ For $a\\ge0$, suppose that $v$, the indexed $r_i$, and $J$ can be evaluated uniformly in $\\mathsf F_{a}$ over prime fields with $$\\label{eq:char-prime-bound}\n p>n(\\ell-1)+\\ell L_0+D^2+10.$$ Additional short, polynomial-time computable lower cutoffs on $p$ are allowed. Then, for every $0\\le d\\le D$, $$[y^d]\\det(yI-M_v)\\pmod p$$ is uniformly computable in $\\mathsf F_{a+8}$ in this prime range. All running-time and query-length bounds are polynomial in the length of the input, including $p$ and the coefficient index $d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-char-coeff",
          "exact_quote": "Consider fixed families $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v\\in\\mathbb Z[x]$, indexed by a word of length $N$. Put $$f_i=x_i^\\ell-r_i,\\qquad\n J=\\det(\\partial_jf_i),\\qquad\n D=\\ell^n,\\qquad L_0=D\\Omega+n(\\ell-1).$$ For $a\\ge0$, suppose that $v$, the indexed $r_i$, and $J$ can be evaluated uniformly in $\\mathsf F_{a}$ over prime fields with $$\\label{eq:char-prime-bound}\n p>n(\\ell-1)+\\ell L_0+D^2+10.$$ Additional short, polynomial-time computable lower cutoffs on $p$ are allowed. Then, for every $0\\le d\\le D$, $$[y^d]\\det(yI-M_v)\\pmod p$$ is uniformly computable in $\\mathsf F_{a+8}$ in this prime range. All running-time and query-length bounds are polynomial in the length of the input, including $p$ and the coefficient index $d$.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider fixed families $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v\\in\\mathbb Z[x]$, indexed by a word of length $N$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\\ell\\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. Suppose that $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v$ are controlled integer polynomial families in variables $x=(x_1,\\ldots,x_n)$ and a polynomially bounded number of parameter variables $t$, uniformly in $i$, and that $\\deg_x r_i<\\ell$. In the monomial basis of $$\\mathbb Z[t,x]/(x_1^\\ell-r_1,\\ldots,x_n^\\ell-r_n),$$ let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$. Then $$\\label{eq:controlled-characteristic}\n Q(t,y)=\\det(yI-M_v)$$ is a controlled family, monic of $y$-degree $D=\\ell^n$. If $d$ and $2^b$ are common total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds on the $r_i,v$, and $T=d+n(\\ell-1)$, valid total-degree and log-norm bounds on $Q$ are $$\\label{eq:determinant-size-bounds}\n d_Q=D\\bigl(1+d(1+T)\\bigr),\\qquad\n b_Q=D^2+D\\bigl(1+b(1+T)\\bigr).$$ The construction is compatible with specializing any parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\\ell\\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. Suppose that $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v$ are controlled integer polynomial families in variables $x=(x_1,\\ldots,x_n)$ and a polynomially bounded number of parameter variables $t$, uniformly in $i$, and that $\\deg_x r_i<\\ell$. In the monomial basis of $$\\mathbb Z[t,x]/(x_1^\\ell-r_1,\\ldots,x_n^\\ell-r_n),$$ let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$. Then $$\\label{eq:controlled-characteristic}\n Q(t,y)=\\det(yI-M_v)$$ is a controlled family, monic of $y$-degree $D=\\ell^n$. If $d$ and $2^b$ are common total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds on the $r_i,v$, and $T=d+n(\\ell-1)$, valid total-degree and log-norm bounds on $Q$ are $$\\label{eq:determinant-size-bounds}\n d_Q=D\\bigl(1+d(1+T)\\bigr),\\qquad\n b_Q=D^2+D\\bigl(1+b(1+T)\\bigr).$$ The construction is compatible with specializing any parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $n\\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\\ell\\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. If $d$ and $2^b$ are common total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds on the $r_i,v$, and $T=d+n(\\ell-1)$, valid total-degree and log-norm bounds on $Q$ are $$\\label{eq:determinant-size-bounds}\n d_Q=D\\bigl(1+d(1+T)\\bigr),\\qquad\n b_Q=D^2+D\\bigl(1+b(1+T)\\bigr).$$ The construction is compatible with specializing any parameters.",
      "explanation": "The multiplication characteristic polynomial is controlled with explicit total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds, compatibly with parameter specialization.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\\ell\\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. If $d$ and $2^b$ are common total-degree and coefficient-norm bounds on the $r_i,v$, and $T=d+n(\\ell-1)$, valid total-degree and log-norm bounds on $Q$ are $$\\label{eq:determinant-size-bounds}\n d_Q=D\\bigl(1+d(1+T)\\bigr),\\qquad\n b_Q=D^2+D\\bigl(1+b(1+T)\\bigr).$$ The construction is compatible with specializing any parameters.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-root-labels",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P(t)$ and $b_0(t)$ be controlled families of integer univariate polynomials, indexed by the same input word. There is a uniformly controlled family $L_a(t)\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$, with labels $a$ of polynomial binary length in a polynomial-time specified range, such that whenever $P\\ne0$:*\n\n1.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, either $L_a(\\alpha)\\ge\\tfrac12$ or $L_a(\\alpha)\\le-\\tfrac12$.*\n\n2.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, if $L_a(\\alpha)>0$, then $b_0(\\alpha)>0$.*\n\n3.  *For every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$ with $b_0(\\alpha)>0$, some label $a$ satisfies $L_a(\\alpha)>0$ and $L_a(\\beta)<0$ at every distinct real root $\\beta$ of $P$.*\n\n*The construction is defined and controlled also when $P=0$, but no root-selection property is asserted in that case. It uses a fixed number of applications of the controlled-family rules, independent of the degrees. Taking $b_0=1$ permits every real root to be selected.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-root-labels",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P(t)$ and $b_0(t)$ be controlled families of integer univariate polynomials, indexed by the same input word. There is a uniformly controlled family $L_a(t)\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$, with labels $a$ of polynomial binary length in a polynomial-time specified range, such that whenever $P\\ne0$:*\n\n1.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, either $L_a(\\alpha)\\ge\\tfrac12$ or $L_a(\\alpha)\\le-\\tfrac12$.*\n\n2.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, if $L_a(\\alpha)>0$, then $b_0(\\alpha)>0$.*\n\n3.  *For every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$ with $b_0(\\alpha)>0$, some label $a$ satisfies $L_a(\\alpha)>0$ and $L_a(\\beta)<0$ at every distinct real root $\\beta$ of $P$.*\n\n*The construction is defined and controlled also when $P=0$, but no root-selection property is asserted in that case. It uses a fixed number of applications of the controlled-family rules, independent of the degrees. Taking $b_0=1$ permits every real root to be selected.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P(t)$ and $b_0(t)$ be controlled families of integer univariate polynomials, indexed by the same input word. There is a uniformly controlled family $L_a(t)\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$, with labels $a$ of polynomial binary length in a polynomial-time specified range, such that whenever $P\\ne0$:*\n\n1.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, either $L_a(\\alpha)\\ge\\tfrac12$ or $L_a(\\alpha)\\le-\\tfrac12$.*\n\n2.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, if $L_a(\\alpha)>0$, then $b_0(\\alpha)>0$.*\n\n3.  *For every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$ with $b_0(\\alpha)>0$, some label $a$ satisfies $L_a(\\alpha)>0$ and $L_a(\\beta)<0$ at every distinct real root $\\beta$ of $P$.*\n\n*The construction is defined and controlled also when $P=0$, but no root-selection property is asserted in that case. It uses a fixed number of applications of the controlled-family rules, independent of the degrees. Taking $b_0=1$ permits every real root to be selected.",
      "explanation": "Short discrete labels can select any prescribed real root satisfying an auxiliary positivity test, including roots with multiplicity. The zero polynomial has no selection guarantee.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-root-labels",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P(t)$ and $b_0(t)$ be controlled families of integer univariate polynomials, indexed by the same input word. There is a uniformly controlled family $L_a(t)\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$, with labels $a$ of polynomial binary length in a polynomial-time specified range, such that whenever $P\\ne0$:*\n\n1.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, either $L_a(\\alpha)\\ge\\tfrac12$ or $L_a(\\alpha)\\le-\\tfrac12$.*\n\n2.  *For every label $a$ and every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$, if $L_a(\\alpha)>0$, then $b_0(\\alpha)>0$.*\n\n3.  *For every real root $\\alpha$ of $P$ with $b_0(\\alpha)>0$, some label $a$ satisfies $L_a(\\alpha)>0$ and $L_a(\\beta)<0$ at every distinct real root $\\beta$ of $P$.*\n\n*The construction is defined and controlled also when $P=0$, but no root-selection property is asserted in that case.           "source_id": "PM-141.1"

      "id": "C12",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [],

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-value-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$ be nonzero, with $\\deg P\\le d$ and $\\left\\lVert P\\right\\rVert_1\\le2^b$, where $d\\ge2$ and $b\\ge1$ are integers. If $g\\in\\mathbb Z[t]$ satisfies $\\deg g\\le d$ and $\\left\\lVert g\\right\\rVert_1\\le2^{b+d^2}$, put $$B_0=b+d^2+d(b+1),\\qquad\n B=d(bd+B_0)+bd+2.$$ At every complex root $\\alpha$ of $P$, either $g(\\alpha)=0$ or $$2^{-B}\\le |g(\\alpha)|\\le2^B.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        {

          "exact_quote": "For a nonzero real polynomial $P$ of degree at most $d$, distinct real roots have distinct strings $$\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}P^{(j)}(\\alpha)\\bigr)_{1\\le j\\le d}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C14",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-thom-separation"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rational-sign",
        "exact_quote": "For integers $B\\ge1$ and $s\\ge1$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n C_{B,s}(z)\n   &=\\prod_{k=-B}^{B}\n       \\bigl(2^{\\max(0,k)}+2^{\\max(0,-k)}z\\bigr)^{2s},\\\\\n A_{B,s}(z)\n   &=\\frac{C_{B,s}(z)-C_{B,s}(-z)}\n           {C_{B,s}(z)+C_{B,s}(-z)}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The denominator is at least $1$ for every real $z$, and $A_{B,s}(0)=0$. For $2^{-B}\\le|z|\\le2^B$, $$|A_{B,s}(z)-\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}z|\\le2\\cdot3^{-2s}.$$ Moreover $C_{B,s}$ is an integer polynomial with $$\\deg C_{B,s}=2s(2B+1),\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert C_{B,s}\\right\\rVert_1\\le2^{2s(2B+1)(B+1)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-controlled-existential",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that an input word specifies a conjunction of polynomially many equations and strict inequalities $$A_i(x)=0,\\qquad B_j(x)>0,$$ in polynomially many real variables. Assume that the numbers of variables and tests, and the type of each addressed test, are computable in polynomial time, and that the polynomials form controlled families uniformly in their indices. Then the existence of a real solution is an available decision. Its counting level depends only on the fixed family constructions and their evaluation algorithms, not on the input or any short outer indices.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-controlled-existential",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that an input word specifies a conjunction of polynomially many equations and strict inequalities $$A_i(x)=0,\\qquad B_j(x)>0,$$ in polynomially many real variables. Assume that the numbers of variables and tests, and the type of each addressed test, are computable in polynomial time, and that the polynomials form controlled families uniformly in their indices. Then the existence of a real solution is an available decision. Its counting level depends only on the fixed family constructions and their evaluation algorithms, not on the input or any short outer indices.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that an input word specifies a conjunction of polynomially many equations and strict inequalities $$A_i(x)=0,\\qquad B_j(x)>0,$$ in polynomially many real variables.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-minimum",
        "exact_quote": "For the nonnegative polynomial $F$ and deformation in Equation <a href=\"#eq:existential-deformation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-deformation\">[eq:existential-deformation]</a>, the minimum $$m(u)=\\min_{x\\in\\mathbb R^n}h_u(x)$$ exists for every real $u\\ge0$. The function $m$ is continuous and nondecreasing, and $Q(u,m(u))=0$. Furthermore, $$F\\text{ has a real zero}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n m(u)\\text{ remains bounded as }u\\to+\\infty.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-existential-discriminant",
        "exact_quote": "The polynomial $Q(0,y)$ has $D$ distinct complex roots. If $\\left\\lVert Q\\right\\rVert_1\\le2^H$, where $H\\ge0$ is an integer, there is a nonzero polynomial $\\Delta\\in\\mathbb Z[u]$ such that every parameter where $Q(u,y)$ has a repeated complex root is a zero of $\\Delta$, and $$\\label{eq:existential-discriminant-bound}\n \\left\\lVert\\Delta\\right\\rVert_1\\le2^{H_\\Delta},\\qquad\n H_\\Delta=(2D)^2+2D(H+D+1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-existential-discriminant",
          "exact_quote": "The polynomial $Q(0,y)$ has $D$ distinct complex roots.           "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The polynomial $Q(0,y)$ has $D$ distinct complex roots.       "explanation": "Separated unperturbed critical values make the exceptional-parameter determinant nonzero and bound its coefficient norm.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ be nonnegative on $\\mathbb R^n$, let $n\\ge1$, and choose an odd $\\ell\\ge3$ with $\\ell>\\deg F$. Define $G,h_u,f_i,Q,D$ by Equations <a href=\"#eq:existential-deformation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-deformation\">[eq:existential-deformation]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:existential-characteristic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-characteristic\">[eq:existential-characteristic]</a>. Let $H,V$ be nonnegative integers bounding $\\log_2\\left\\lVert Q\\right\\rVert_1$ and $\\deg_uQ$, respectively, and choose the parameters in Equation <a href=\"#eq:existential-specialization-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-specialization-parameters\">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>. Then $$q(y)=Q(U,y)$$ is monic and squarefree, has degree $D$ and coefficient norm at most $2^K$, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:existential-root-equivalence}\n F\\text{ has a real zero}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n q\\text{ has a real root less than }R.$$ Every real root of $q$ is the value of $h_U$ at a real common zero of $f_1(\\cdot,U),\\ldots,f_n(\\cdot,U)$.*\n\n*When $F$ varies in a controlled family, $\\ell$ is chosen from its degree bound as above, and $H,V$ are polynomial-time computable short bounds, $q$ is controlled. All remaining bound parameters in Equation <a href=\"#eq:existential-specialization-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-specialization-parameters\">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>, including $D,H_\\Delta$, are computable in polynomial time and have polynomial binary length.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ be nonnegative on $\\mathbb R^n$, let $n\\ge1$, and choose an odd $\\ell\\ge3$ with $\\ell>\\deg F$.           "source_id": "PM-141.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_n]$ be nonnegative on $\\mathbb R^n$, let $n\\ge1$, and choose an odd $\\ell\\ge3$ with $\\ell>\\deg F$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quartic-fibers",
        "exact_quote": "From a well-formed input of length $N$, one can construct in polynomial time an explicitly listed polynomial $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_r,z_1,\\ldots,z_n]$, with $n\\ge1$ polynomially bounded in $N$, such that $F\\ge0$ on real inputs, $\\deg F\\le4$, and, for every $x\\in\\mathbb R^r$, $$\\label{eq:fiber-encoding}\n \\exists y\\in\\mathbb R^s\\;\\neg\\Phi(x,y)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\exists z\\in\\mathbb R^n\\;F(x,z)=0.$$ The monomial count and coefficient bit lengths of $F$ are polynomially bounded in $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-141.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-quartic-fibers",
          "exact_quote": "From a well-formed input of length $N$, one can construct in polynomial time an explicitly listed polynomial $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_r,z_1,\\ldots,z_n]$, with $n\\ge1$ polynomially bounded in $N$, such that $F\\ge0$ on real inputs, $\\deg F\\le4$, and, for every $x\\in\\mathbb R^r$, $$\\label{eq:fiber-encoding}\n \\exists y\\in\\mathbb R^s\\;\\neg\\Phi(x,y)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\exists z\\in\\mathbb R^n\\;F(x,z)=0.$$ The monomial count and coefficient bit lengths of $F$ are polynomially bounded in $N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "From a well-formed input of length $N$, one can construct in polynomial time an explicitly listed polynomial $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x_1,\\ldots,x_r,z_1,\\ldots,z_n]$, with $n\\ge1$ polynomially bounded in $N$, such that $F\\ge0$ on real inputs, $\\deg F\\le4$, and, for every $x\\in\\mathbb R^r$, $$\\label{eq:fiber-encoding}\n \\exists y\\in\\mathbb R^s\\;\\neg\\Phi(x,y)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\exists z\\in\\mathbb R^n\\;F(x,z)=0.$$ The monomial count and coefficient bit lengths of $F$ are polynomially bounded in $N$.",
      "explanation": "Arithmetic and Boolean wire constraints give an explicitly listed nonnegative quartic encoding counterexamples separately in every parameter fiber.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $W\\in\\mathbb R[c_1,\\ldots,c_k]$ be nonnegative on $\\mathbb R^k$, where $k\\ge1$, and let $C$ be a nonempty subset of $Z=\\{W=0\\}$ that is both open and closed in $Z$. There exists $c^*\\in C$ such that $$P_{i,j_i}(c_i^*)=0\\qquad(1\\le i\\le k).$$ If $W$ is a controlled family of integer polynomials, $k$ is polynomially bounded, and $\\ell$ is short and computable in polynomial time, then the polynomials $P_i$ and $P_{i,j}$ are uniformly controlled.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Controlled polynomial family** ([source](#stmt-def-controlled)): A family $V_w\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_{n(w)}]$ is *controlled* if the following conditions hold. 1. The number $n(w)$ is polynomially bounded and computable in polynomial time. 2. Nonnegative short integers $d(w),b(w),\lambda(w)$ are computable in polynomial time and satisfy $$\deg V_w\le d(w),\qquad \left\lVert V_w\right\rVert_1\le2^{b(w)}.$$ The zero polynomial is permitted and satisfies every nonnegative degree bound. 3. For some fixed $a$, evaluation $$(w,p,z)\longmapsto V_w(z)\bmod p, \qquad z\in\mathbb F_p^{n(w)},$$ is in $\mathsf F_{a}$ on prime inputs $p>\lambda(w)$. Field elements are given by their least nonnegative binary representatives. The integers $d,b,\lambda$ depend only on $w$, not on the evaluation prime or evaluation point.
- **Available function** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We call a function *available* if it belongs to $\mathsf F_{a}$ for some fixed $a$.
- **Short integer or index** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An integer or index is called *short* if its binary encoding has length bounded by a fixed polynomial in the relevant input length.
- **Existential real reduction class** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\exists\mathbb R$ for the class of languages polynomial-time many-one reducible to $\mathsf{ETR}$.
- **Coefficient norm** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an integer polynomial $V$, write $\left\lVert V\right\rVert_1$ for the sum of the absolute values of all its coefficients, in all its indeterminates.
- **Pure-power quotient algebra** ([source](#eq:quotient-algebra)): Set $$\label{eq:quotient-algebra} f_i=x_i^\ell-r_i,\qquad \mathcal A=S[x_1,\ldots,x_n]/(f_1,\ldots,f_n),\qquad D=\ell^n,\qquad \tau=(\ell-1,\ldots,\ell-1).$$
- **Formal coefficient functional** ([source](#eq:laurent-functional)): Define the $S$-linear functional $$\label{eq:laurent-functional} \mathcal L(v)= [x_1^{-1}\cdots x_n^{-1}] v(x)\prod_{i=1}^n \left(x_i^{-\ell}\sum_{j\ge0} (r_i(x)x_i^{-\ell})^j\right).$$
- **Closed degree lift** ([source](#pm-h-25)): To make the separation in Proposition <a href="#prop:degree-pieces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:degree-pieces">23</a> usable in a compact minimization argument, introduce one real variable $t$ and define $$\label{eq:closed-lift} W_d(x,t)=(E_d(x)t-1)^2+\sum_{j=d+1}^{d_E}E_j(x)^2, \qquad Z_d=\{(x,t)\in\mathbb R^{r+1}:W_d(x,t)=0\}.$$

| Q1 | Sufficient counting level for existential real feasibility | ≤ | $26$ | The circuit encoding of the existential theory of the reals used in the paper. |
| Q2 | Monomial-basis rank | = | $\ell^n$ | Pure-power relations with lower total-degree remainders. |
| Q3 | Total-degree bound for the multiplication characteristic polynomial | = | $D\bigl(1+d(1+T)\bigr)$ | Common total-degree bound and pure-power quotient rank. |
| Q4 | Logarithmic coefficient-norm bound for the multiplication characteristic polynomial | = | $D^2+D\bigl(1+b(1+T)\bigr)$ | Common coefficient-norm bound and pure-power quotient rank. |
| Q5 | Degree of the rational-sign product | = | $2s(2B+1)$ | Positive integer range and even-power parameters. |
| Q6 | Coefficient-norm bound for the rational-sign product | ≤ | $2^{2s(2B+1)(B+1)}$ | Positive integer range and even-power parameters. |
| Q7 | Uniform rational-sign error | ≤ | $2\cdot3^{-2s}$ | Nonzero real arguments within the stated dyadic range. |
| Q8 | Number of sparse derivative-sign conditions | ≤ | $\lceil\log_2\|S\|\rceil$ | A nonempty set of distinct real roots of a nonzero real polynomial. |
| Q9 | CRT prime-window endpoint | = | $2Z^4$ | The maximum of the source magnitude, cutoff and baseline parameters. |
| Q10 | Circular rounding error | < | $2^{-10}$ | The dyadic rounding of the prime-residue sum in the integer-sign construction. |
| Q11 | Sufficient finite-field product evaluation level | ≤ | $a+4$ | Uniform prime-field factors with polynomial-length indices. |
| Q12 | Sufficient characteristic-coefficient evaluation level | ≤ | $a+8$ | Prime fields satisfying the stated degree and denominator cutoff. |

            "anchor": "stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant",
            "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, and let $\\ell\\ge2$ be a short polynomial-time computable integer. Suppose that $r_1,\\ldots,r_n,v$ are controlled integer polynomial families in variables $x=(x_1,\\ldots,x_n)$ and a polynomially bounded number of parameter variables $t$, uniformly in $i$, and that $\\deg_x r_i<\\ell$. In the monomial basis of $$\\mathbb Z[t,x]/(x_1^\\ell-r_1,\\ldots,x_n^\\ell-r_n),$$ let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$. Then $$\\label{eq:controlled-characteristic}\n Q(t,y)=\\det(yI-M_v)$$ is a controlled family, monic of $y$-degree $D=\\ell^n$. Suppose that $n\\ge1$ is polynomially bounded and polynomial-time computable, that $\\ell\\ge2$ and $\\Omega\\ge0$ are short polynomial-time computable integers, and that $\\deg_x r_i<\\ell$ and $\\deg_x v\\le\\Omega$. Put $$f_i=x_i^\\ell-r_i,\\qquad\n J=\\det(\\partial_jf_i),\\qquad\n D=\\ell^n,\\qquad L_0=D\\Omega+n(\\ell-1).$$ For $a\\ge0$, suppose that $v$, the indexed $r_i$, and $J$ can be evaluated uniformly in $\\mathsf F_{a}$ over prime fields with $$\\label{eq:char-prime-bound}\n p>n(\\ell-1)+\\ell L_0+D^2+10.$$ Additional short, polynomial-time computable lower cutoffs on $p$ are allowed. Then, for every $0\\le d\\le D$, $$[y^d]\\det(yI-M_v)\\pmod p$$ is uniformly computable in $\\mathsf F_{a+8}$ in this prime range. All running-time and query-length bounds are polynomial in the length of the input, including $p$ and the coefficient index $d$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-controlled-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-controlled-closure): Combine short-index counting and finite-field evaluation while computing uniform bounds before choosing a prime. -> Controlled families under fixed algebraic constructions
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-crt-sign) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-crt-sign): Approximate the residue quotient on the circle and determine the sign at the first flagged doubling index. -> Available signs of controlled integers
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-multiplication-determinant): Use the formal trace identity, coefficient extraction and truncated exponentiation instead of building the large matrix. -> Controlled multiplication characteristic polynomials
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-root-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-sign-2): Fingerprint derivative signs with modular weights, approximate signs and clear positive denominators. -> Short labels selecting prescribed real roots
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-specialization): Replace boundedness of a coercive minimum by a specialization beyond all crossing and repeated-root parameters. -> A squarefree univariate real-root threshold test
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-controlled-existential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-specialization-2): Select a root below the threshold and detect its sign through a shifted multiplication determinant. -> Available controlled existential feasibility
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-degree-pieces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-degree-pieces): Encode universal counterexamples fiberwise, substitute the growth power and separate actual-degree pieces. -> A closed lift whose good part is relatively open and closed
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-candidate-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-candidate-coordinates): Use compact penalty minimizers and highest parameter coefficients to supply witness coordinate polynomials. -> Coordinate-root candidates in the selected part
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-27): Guess short coordinate indices and labels, then test nonemptiness and absence of fiber counterexamples. -> The fixed counting-level decision for existential–universal real sentences
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-etr-bound) / [proof](#pm-h-31): Replace prescribed-root labels by sparse derivative conditions and account for the fixed sequence of oracle levels. -> The explicit sufficient counting level for the existential fragment

Define $G,h_u,f_i,Q,D$ by Equations <a href=\"#eq:existential-deformation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-deformation\">[eq:existential-deformation]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:existential-characteristic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-characteristic\">[eq:existential-characteristic]</a>. Let $H,V$ be nonnegative integers bounding $\\log_2\\left\\lVert Q\\right\\rVert_1$ and $\\deg_uQ$, respectively, and choose the parameters in Equation <a href=\"#eq:existential-specialization-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-specialization-parameters\">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>. Then $$q(y)=Q(U,y)$$ is monic and squarefree, has degree $D$ and coefficient norm at most $2^K$, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:existential-root-equivalence}\n F\\text{ has a real zero}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n q\\text{ has a real root less than }R.$$ Every real root of $q$ is the value of $h_U$ at a real common zero of $f_1(\\cdot,U),\\ldots,f_n(\\cdot,U)$.*\n\n*When $F$ varies in a controlled family, $\\ell$ is chosen from its degree bound as above, and $H,V$ are polynomial-time computable short bounds, $q$ is controlled. All remaining bound parameters in Equation <a href=\"#eq:existential-specialization-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:existential-specialization-parameters\">[eq:existential-specialization-parameters]</a>, including $D,H_\\Delta$, are computable in polynomial time and have polynomial binary length.",
          "source_id": "PM-141.1"
        }
      ],

Choose an odd integer $\\ell\\ge3$ with $\\ell>\\deg W$, and put $$G(c)=\\sum_{i=1}^k c_i^{\\ell+1},\\qquad\n H_w(c)=G(c)+(\\ell+1)wW(c).$$ In the quotient by $$\\label{eq:candidate-relations}\n c_i^\\ell+w\\frac{\\partial W}{\\partial c_i}=0\n \\qquad(1\\le i\\le k),$$ form the coordinate multiplication polynomials $$\\label{eq:coordinate-polynomials}\n P_i(w,T)=\\det(TI-M_{c_i})=\\sum_j P_{i,j}(T)w^j.$$ For each $i$, let $j_i$ be the largest index for which $P_{i,j_i}\\ne0$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-exact**: The input language uses exact operations and comparisons on explicitly encoded circuits.
- **I-depth**: Controlled-family closure requires a fixed number of constructions.
- **I-sign**: The rational sign approximation has a positive denominator, vanishes at zero, and has the stated error on the specified range.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-exact**: Substitute floating-point feasibility checks for exact real sentence truth. -> Approximate signs do not implement the stipulated exact interpretation.
- **A-depth**: Iterate controlled-family constructions to an input-dependent depth while retaining a fixed counting level. -> The closure proposition does not supply such a level guarantee.
- **A-range**: Use the sign approximation error estimate for nonzero values outside its dyadic range. -> The stated estimate does not cover those values.
- **A-simple**: Realize every real characteristic root as a real common zero without checking simplicity. -> The real-eigenline implication requires a simple real root.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite product in the rational sign approximation is constructive and permits exact evaluation on rational inputs. This implementation checks that construction; it does not implement the real-sentence decision procedure.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON integers B,s and a list of exact rational strings zs.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= B <= 3; 1 <= s <= 3; at most 129 rational points of bounded encoding length.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational approximant values, polynomial degree and coefficient norm.
# TIME: Polynomial arithmetic and evaluation on the explicitly expanded finite product; no complexity-theorem implementation.
# SPACE: Linear in the expanded polynomial degree, apart from exact coefficient bit lengths.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer polynomials and rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid contracts raise an exception; no floating-point fallback.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpz_poly,fmpq
v=json.load(sys.stdin)
B,s=v['B'],v['s']
assert type(B) is int and type(s) is int and 1<=B<=3 and 1<=s<=3
assert isinstance(v['zs'],list) and len(v['zs'])<=129
C=fmpz_poly([1])
for k in range(-B,B+1):
    C *= fmpz_poly([2**max(0,k),2**max(0,-k)])**(2*s)
vals=[]
for raw in v['zs']:
    assert isinstance(raw,str) and len(raw)<=32
    z=fmpq(raw)
    assert z==0 or fmpq(1,2**B)<=abs(z)<=2**B
    def ev(t):
        acc=fmpq(0)
        for coefficient in reversed(C.coeffs()):
            acc=acc*t+coefficient
        return acc
    pos,neg=ev(z),ev(-z)
    assert pos+neg>=1
    vals.append(str((pos-neg)/(pos+neg)))
print(json.dumps({'values':vals,'degree':C.degree(),'norm':str(sum(abs(c) for c in C.coeffs()))}))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Exact symbolic decision software can adapt the circuit input discipline and the reductions to polynomial feasibility. The hierarchy result supplies a theoretical decision bound, without a practical solver performance guarantee. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no game dynamics, simulation error model or time-stepping contract is supplied. The circuit language describes exact polynomial conditions. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Symbolic algebra tooling can adapt coefficient extraction, specialization and real-root labeling while retaining degree and coefficient bounds. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper supplies uniform reductions for real feasibility and quantified sentences using fixed-depth family constructions, modular evaluations and determinant signs. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A polynomial descriptor retaining bounds and modular evaluation supports implicit algebraic manipulation without full coefficient expansion. This is a representation contract rather than a general-purpose storage benchmark. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no accelerator architecture, memory layout or hardware throughput contract is supplied. Counting-level evaluation is an abstract computational bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no security model, cryptographic protocol or hardness assumption is supplied. The prime-field evaluation contract serves exact algebraic computation. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no learning objective, statistical model or training guarantee is supplied. The stated decisions concern exact polynomial sentences. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | Coercive polynomial penalties relate real zero-set feasibility to bounded minima and algebraic critical values. This supplies an exact feasibility construction, without a numerical optimization convergence contract. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no network, failure, consistency or communication model is supplied. Uniform evaluation refers to fixed computation procedures. |
| other (computational complexity of real algebraic decision problems) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The main contribution is a counting-hierarchy upper bound for exact real quantification, supported by controlled polynomial constructions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-encoding** (formal_scope): The language uses explicitly listed acyclic arithmetic circuits with signed binary integer constants and the stated arithmetic gates. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem directly to circuits with division or exponentiation gates.']
- **limit-fixed_nesting** (hypothesis): The controlled-family closure rule requires fixed nesting of counting constructions. Blocked use: ['Claiming closure under an input-dependent sequence of successive counting operations.']
- **limit-adaptive_access** (formal_scope): The available-function argument permits adaptive calls without asserting a collapse of adaptive access to the probabilistic counting class. Blocked use: ['Using the closure argument to claim that adaptive oracle access collapses to its oracle class.']
- **limit-zero_root_polynomial** (edge_case): The root-label construction remains defined for the zero polynomial, but its root-selection guarantee does not apply there. Blocked use: ['Using labels to isolate a prescribed root of the zero polynomial.']
- **limit-simple_real_root** (hypothesis): Realization of a real characteristic root by a real common zero uses real coefficients and simplicity of that root. Blocked use: ['Inferring a real common zero from a repeated real characteristic root by this lemma alone.']
- **limit-sign_range** (approximation): The rational sign error guarantee applies on the stated dyadic range; the zero value is handled separately. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated uniform error bound to nonzero values outside its specified range.']
- **limit-degree_topology** (formal_scope): Goodness is locally constant within each degree piece; the pieces need not be closed in the ambient space. Blocked use: ['Replacing the relative-topology assertion by ambient closedness of every degree piece.']
- **limit-finite_depth_not_runtime** (formal_scope): Fixed counting-level membership does not supply a short explicit expansion of the intermediate polynomials. Blocked use: ['Treating the complexity containment as a guarantee that full coefficient lists can be constructed efficiently.']
- **limit-minimum_hypothesis** (hypothesis): The bounded-minimum criterion uses a nonnegative polynomial and a coercive perturbation. Blocked use: ['Applying that criterion to an arbitrary signed polynomial without establishing the hypotheses.']
- **limit-candidate_part** (hypothesis): Candidate coordinates are guaranteed for a nonempty part of the zero set that is both open and closed within that zero set. Blocked use: ['Applying the coordinate construction to an arbitrary selected subset of the zero set.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the existential theory of the reals lies in the counting hierarchy. More generally, we show that the truth of existential–universal sentences over the reals can be decided in a fixed level of the counting hierarchy, even when the integer polynomials are given by arithmetic circuits.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:input-sentence"></a>
A sentence with one existential and one universal real block asks whether there is a real tuple for which every real tuple in a second block satisfies specified polynomial conditions. We study the discrete language $\exists\forall\mathbb R_{\mathrm{circ}}$ of true sentences $$\label{eq:input-sentence}
 \exists x\in\mathbb R^r\ \forall y\in\mathbb R^s:\ \Phi(x,y).$$ Here $r,s\ge0$ are represented by explicit lists of variables, and $\Phi$ is an explicitly encoded Boolean formula using AND, OR, and NOT over atoms $p=0$ and $p>0$. Each $p$ is given by an explicitly listed acyclic arithmetic circuit with signed binary integer constants and gates $+,-,\times$. Predecessor indices specify the gates; sharing is allowed, and there are no division or exponentiation gates. All operations and comparisons in <a href="#eq:input-sentence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:input-sentence">[eq:input-sentence]</a> are interpreted exactly. The input length $N$ is the total binary encoding length, and malformed encodings are rejected.

All computational bounds below are uniform bit-time bounds. Let $\mathsf{PP}$ denote polynomial-time probabilistic computation with fair random bits, accepting exactly the yes-instances with probability greater than $1/2$. The counting hierarchy, introduced by Wagner (Wagner 1986) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Wagner1986](#ref-Wagner1986)], is defined by $$\mathsf C_{0}\mathsf P=\mathsf P,\qquad
 \mathsf C_{j+1}\mathsf P=\bigcup_{A\in\mathsf C_{j}\mathsf P}\mathsf{PP}^A,
 \qquad \mathsf{CH}=\bigcup_{j\ge0}\mathsf C_{j}\mathsf P.$$ Membership uses a fixed oracle language and a fixed uniform machine.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is an absolute integer $j$ such that $$\exists\forall\mathbb R_{\mathrm{circ}}\in\mathsf C_{j}\mathsf P.$$ The level $j$ is independent of the input length, the numbers of variables, the degrees of the circuit polynomials, and their coefficient magnitudes.*

</div>

The existential theory of the reals, denoted $\mathsf{ETR}$, is the same decision problem with only the existential block. Write $\exists\mathbb R$ for the class of languages polynomial-time many-one reducible to $\mathsf{ETR}$. Finer accounting for this fragment gives $\mathsf{ETR}\in\mathsf C_{26}\mathsf P$ and hence $\exists\mathbb R\subseteq\mathsf C_{26}\mathsf P$; Theorem <a href="#thm:etr-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:etr-bound">25</a> proves this explicit bound. The main result allows a universal block of unrestricted dimension as well.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

Tarski proved decidability of the first-order theory of real closed fields (Tarski 1951) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Tarski1951](#ref-Tarski1951)]. Collins’s cylindrical algebraic decomposition gave a geometric method for eliminating quantifiers (Collins 1975) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Collins1975](#ref-Collins1975)], and Canny established the polynomial-space upper bound for the existential fragment (Canny 1988) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Canny1988](#ref-Canny1988)]. For each fixed number of real quantifier blocks, Renegar’s decision bounds yield a polynomial-space algorithm (Renegar 1992, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Renegar1992II](#ref-Renegar1992II)]. This applies to the circuit encoding above: the quartic reduction in Lemma <a href="#lem:quartic-fibers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:quartic-fibers">22</a> preserves the two quantifier blocks. Our theorem places this two-block language in a fixed level of the counting hierarchy. Real quantifier hierarchies distinguish the number and order of blocks; Schaefer and Štefankovič develop their finite-encoding formulation (Schaefer and Štefankovič 2024) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[SchaeferStefankovic2025](#ref-SchaeferStefankovic2025)]. The existential class already captures many geometric realization and continuous feasibility problems; Schaefer, Cardinal, and Miltzow survey these connections (Schaefer, Cardinal, and Miltzow 2026) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[SchaeferCardinalMiltzow2026](#ref-SchaeferCardinalMiltzow2026)].

The counting hierarchy gives a way to decide some exact algebraic questions without constructing their enormous intermediate integers. Allender, Bürgisser, Kjeldgaard-Pedersen, and Miltersen proved that $\mathsf{PosSLP}$—positivity of the integer output of a division-free straight-line program—belongs to $\mathsf{CH}$ (Allender et al. 2009) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[AllenderBurgisserKjeldgaardPedersenMiltersen2009](#ref-AllenderBurgisserKjeldgaardPedersenMiltersen2009)]. Their modular-product and approximate Chinese-remainder techniques are the counting tools behind our treatment of implicit polynomial families. Every fixed counting level lies in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$: a depth-first simulation enumerates random choices and answers the fixed-depth oracle queries using polynomial space.

Recent counting-hierarchy bounds address other polynomial feasibility problems. Andrews, Garg, and Schost place Hilbert’s Nullstellensatz over $\mathbb Q$ and several other fields in $\mathsf{CH}$ (Andrews, Garg, and Schost 2026) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[AndrewsGargSchost2026](#ref-AndrewsGargSchost2026)]; the common zeros there lie in an algebraic closure. Balaji, Shirmohammadi, Tavenas, and Worrell prove counting-hierarchy bounds for approximate polynomial satisfiability over $\mathbb Q$ and finite fields (Balaji et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BalajiShirmohammadiTavenasWorrell2026](#ref-BalajiShirmohammadiTavenasWorrell2026)], where the question is membership of zero in the Zariski closure of a polynomial image over the algebraic closure. Exact real quantification must also control the reality of solutions, their attainment, and the dependence of a quantified fiber on its parameters. These are the issues addressed by the argument here.

Our algebraic tools belong to the critical-point and deformation methods of real algebraic geometry (Basu, Pollack, and Roy 2006) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BasuPollackRoy2006](#ref-BasuPollackRoy2006)]. Multiplication determinants are evaluated through the classical relation between residues and traces (Scheja and Storch 1975; Cattani, Dickenstein, and Sturmfels 1996) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[SchejaStorch1975](#ref-SchejaStorch1975), [CattaniDickensteinSturmfels1996](#ref-CattaniDickensteinSturmfels1996)]. We prove the needed identities and computational rules directly, including their behavior under parameter specialization. These computational rules apply to implicitly represented families that need not have short coefficient lists or arithmetic circuits.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From quantified fibers to short algebraic witnesses

Fix the existential tuple $x$. Introducing gate and auxiliary variables turns failure of $\Phi(x,y)$ into a real zero of a nonnegative quartic $F(x,z)$, where $z$ includes $y$ and the auxiliaries. Thus we seek an $x$ for which the fiber $F(x,\cdot)$ has no real zero. Call such an $x$ *good*. Merely testing fibers does not provide a finite description of a good parameter. The main geometric argument produces such descriptions.

For $u\ge0$, consider the minimum $$m_x(u)=\min_{z\in\mathbb R^n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^n z_i^6+6uF(x,z)\right).$$ The sixth powers keep bounded sublevel sets compact. It follows that $m_x(u)$ remains bounded as $u\to\infty$ precisely when the fiber has a zero. At a minimizing point the critical equations have pure fifth powers as their leading terms. Their quotient algebra has dimension $D=5^n$, and multiplication by the minimized polynomial has a monic characteristic polynomial $Q(x,u,Y)$ satisfying $Q(x,u,m_x(u))=0$.

This identity supplies a uniform growth test: $x$ is good precisely when $m_x(v^{D+1})>v$ for all sufficiently large $v$. Form $$E(x,v)=Q(x,v^{D+1},v).$$ The coefficient of $v^D$ is always $1$, so $E(x,\cdot)$ is never zero. Partition parameter space into the sets on which $E(x,\cdot)$ has each possible degree. On each such degree piece, polynomial root bounds give a locally uniform threshold beyond which $E$ does not vanish. Continuity of the minimum then makes goodness locally constant on that piece. Introducing the reciprocal of its leading coefficient realizes the piece as a closed algebraic set. Both the good and nongood parts of this lift are closed.

A second minimization argument now extracts candidate coordinates. Choose a compact sublevel set of a coercive polynomial with a good point in its interior. Within this set, the good and nongood parts of the lift are separated. Minimize the coercive polynomial plus a growing nonnegative polynomial penalty that vanishes exactly on the lift, restricting to a compact neighborhood that avoids the nongood part. A subsequence of minimizers converges to a good point and is eventually interior to the neighborhood, so these minimizers satisfy the unconstrained critical equations. The highest parameter coefficients of their coordinate characteristic polynomials therefore vanish at the limit coordinates. This produces finitely indexed univariate polynomial families whose roots contain the coordinates of a suitable witness, without computing the good part or its connected components. Section <a href="#sec:two-blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:two-blocks">6</a> proves this geometric construction and completes the main theorem.

Two computational ingredients make these candidate coordinates usable. First, all polynomials just described have degree and logarithmic coefficient bounds with polynomial-length binary encodings, and their finite-field evaluations lie at fixed counting levels. We call such families *controlled*. Sections <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">2</a> and <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">3</a> establish their closure rules and the evaluation of multiplication determinants without constructing the matrices.

Second, a chosen real root needs a short description even when its polynomial has exponential degree. Derivative signs distinguish real roots, as in Thom encodings (Basu, Pollack, and Roy 2006) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BasuPollackRoy2006](#ref-BasuPollackRoy2006)], but the full sign string can be too long. Section <a href="#sec:labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:labels">4</a> compresses that string to a weighted integer sum with weights computed modulo a short prime, and realizes the resulting label by a polynomial positivity test. Every desired real root can be isolated, including a root of a polynomial with multiplicities. The sign approximation uses geometric products of the type studied by Beigel, Reingold, and Spielman (Beigel, Reingold, and Spielman 1995) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BeigelReingoldSpielman1995](#ref-BeigelReingoldSpielman1995)]. This arbitrary-root labeling rule and the coordinate construction are reusable ingredients of the proof.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:existential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:existential">5</a> proves an existential decision procedure for controlled families. For a guessed coordinate description, we use it twice: the described parameter set must be nonempty, and it must contain no parameter with a zero of $F$. A suitable choice of root labels singles out the good point constructed above. All choices have polynomial total bit length, and all family constructions have fixed depth. Counting over these choices therefore gives one fixed level of the hierarchy.

<a id="sec:counting"></a>
### Counting and controlled polynomial families

The polynomials used below can have exponentially large degrees and coefficients with exponentially many bits. Their full expansions are therefore unsuitable as algorithmic data. We retain instead three pieces of information: a degree bound, a coefficient-norm bound, and a procedure for evaluation modulo a prime. This section develops the counting operations that preserve this description and recover the sign of an integer from it. The modular-product and approximate Chinese-remainder methods follow the arithmetic approach of Allender et al. (Allender et al. 2009, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AllenderBurgisserKjeldgaardPedersenMiltersen2009](#ref-AllenderBurgisserKjeldgaardPedersenMiltersen2009)]; we prove the forms, including the oracle bounds, that we need.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Uniformity and counting

Recall that $\mathsf C_{0}\mathsf P=\mathsf P$ and $\mathsf C_{a+1}\mathsf P=\bigcup_{A\in\mathsf C_{a}\mathsf P}\mathsf{PP}^A$. Put $$\mathsf F_{a}=\bigcup_{A\in\mathsf C_{a}\mathsf P}\mathsf{FP}^A.$$ A function in $\mathsf F_{a}$ has polynomially bounded output length and is computed in polynomial bit time with one fixed oracle in $\mathsf C_{a}\mathsf P$. We call a function *available* if it belongs to $\mathsf F_{a}$ for some fixed $a$. A decision made by such a function belongs to $\mathsf C_{a+1}\mathsf P$: a probabilistic oracle machine can perform the same computation and ignore its random bits.

The levels $\mathsf C_{a}\mathsf P$ are increasing and are closed under finite tagged unions. For the second assertion, induct on $a$, select the machine indicated by the tag, and replace its oracle by the tagged union of the finitely many lower-level oracles. Padding with unused random bits gives one polynomial random-bit bound. Consequently, polynomial-time computation using any fixed finite collection of available functions again gives an available function. This observation permits adaptive calls; it makes no assertion that adaptive access to $\mathsf{PP}$ collapses to $\mathsf{PP}$.

An integer or index is called *short* if its binary encoding has length bounded by a fixed polynomial in the relevant input length. For a tuple, this means its total encoding length. A short integer may have exponentially large value. Every index range below has a fixed polynomial encoding bound, and every machine is fixed independently of the input. All algorithms are total: length and range checks prevent invalid accesses, and undefined arithmetic operations receive fixed defaults. Their asserted algebraic guarantees apply on the specified domains.

<a id="stmt-lem-counting"></a>
<div id="lem:counting" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Counting and summation). *Fix $a\ge0$. Let $b(w)$ and $s(w)$ be polynomially bounded nonnegative integers computable in polynomial time. If $A(w,i)$, for $i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}$, is a predicate computable in $\mathsf F_{a}$, then $$C(w)=\#\{i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}:A(w,i)\}$$ is in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$. Existential and universal quantification over these indices are also in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$.*

*If $f(w,i)$ is in $\mathsf F_{a}$ and $0\le f(w,i)<2^{s(w)}$, then $\sum_i f(w,i)$ is in $\mathsf F_{a+1}$. The same conclusion holds for sums modulo a positive short integer.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-counting"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-counting</summary>

*Proof.* Write $b=b(w)$. To test $C(w)>t$ for $0\le t\le2^b$, use one fair choice bit and $b$ further random bits. On the first choice accept exactly when $A(w,i)$ holds; on the second accept on exactly $2^b-t$ of the $b$-bit strings. There are $$C(w)+2^b-t$$ accepting choices, a strict majority precisely when $C(w)>t$. The computation of $A$ is polynomial time with a fixed $\mathsf C_{a}\mathsf P$ oracle, so the threshold language lies in $\mathsf C_{a+1}\mathsf P$. Binary search recovers $C(w)$, whose encoding uses at most $b+1$ bits. Unused random bits can pad this machine to a fixed polynomial bound without changing its majority test.

The two quantified predicates follow by comparing the count with $0$ or $2^b$. For summation, count pairs $(i,j)$ with $j\in\{0,1\}^{s(w)}$ and $j<f(w,i)$. The resulting integer has at most $b+s(w)+1$ bits. Reduction modulo a short modulus takes polynomial bit time. Restrictions on the indices may be included in the predicate or implemented by zero summands. ◻

</details>

A sum over a tuple of indices uses one application of Lemma <a href="#lem:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:counting">2</a>, as long as the tuple has polynomial total length. In particular, the number of counting levels does not grow with the number of coordinates in a grid.

<a id="stmt-lem-product"></a>
<div id="lem:product" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Products in a finite field). *<span id="lem:field-product" label="lem:field-product"></span>Fix $a\ge0$. Let $p$ be a prime of polynomial bit length and let $b(w)$ be polynomially bounded and computable in polynomial time. Suppose $f(w,p,i)\in\mathbb F_p$, for $i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}$, is computable in $\mathsf F_{a}$. Then $$\prod_{i\in\{0,1\}^{b(w)}} f(w,p,i)\pmod p$$ is computable in $\mathsf F_{a+4}$. A restricted index range can be used by assigning factor $1$ outside it. The algorithm can be made total, with a fixed default when $p$ is not prime.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-product"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-product</summary>

*Proof.* Primality can be tested in $\mathsf F_{1}$ by quantifying over possible divisors. For prime $p$, the multiplicative group $\mathbb F_p^*$ is cyclic. Here is the elementary argument. Let $L$ be the least common multiple of the element orders. For each prime power dividing $L$, a power of some group element has exactly that prime-power order. The product of these elements has order $L$, since the orders are pairwise coprime. Thus $L\le p-1$. Every nonzero field element is a root of $z^L-1$, so $p-1\le L$ by the polynomial root bound. Hence $L=p-1$.

A nonzero $\gamma\in\mathbb F_p$ is a generator precisely when $$\gamma^e\ne1\pmod p\qquad(1\le e\le p-2).$$ Each power is computable in polynomial bit time. By Lemma <a href="#lem:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:counting">2</a>, the universal test is in $\mathsf F_{1}$. An existential search over a short interval of candidates, followed by binary search, therefore finds the least generator in $\mathsf F_{2}$. For $p=2$ the displayed range is empty and $\gamma=1$.

First test whether any factor is zero, in which case the product is zero. Otherwise, each factor has a unique discrete logarithm $e_i\in\{0,\ldots,p-2\}$ satisfying $$\gamma^{e_i}=f(w,p,i)\pmod p.$$ Existential searches over intervals, again followed by binary search, recover $e_i$ in $\mathsf F_{\max(a,2)+1}$. Summation puts $\sum_i e_i$ in $\mathsf F_{\max(a,2)+2}$. This sum is short, since it is less than $2^{b(w)}p$. Reduce it modulo $p-1$ and raise $\gamma$ to the resulting exponent. Since $\max(a,2)+2\le a+4$, all operations, including the zero test and primality check, fit the asserted level. ◻

</details>

If a function receives $p$ as an additional input, its polynomial bounds may use $|w|+\operatorname{bitlength}(p)$. An application on a smaller input, however, must supply $p$ with length polynomial in that smaller input. We will distinguish such evaluation inputs from the data that describe the underlying polynomial family.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Polynomial families accessible by evaluation

Counting and finite-field products allow algebraic constructions without writing their coefficients. We now specify exactly what is retained about a polynomial, and prove that coefficient extraction and differentiation are compatible with this information. For an integer polynomial $V$, write $\left\lVert V\right\rVert_1$ for the sum of the absolute values of all its coefficients, in all its indeterminates.

<a id="stmt-def-controlled"></a>
<div id="def:controlled" class="definition">

**Definition 4** (Controlled polynomial family). A family $V_w\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_{n(w)}]$ is *controlled* if the following conditions hold.

1.  The number $n(w)$ is polynomially bounded and computable in polynomial time.

2.  Nonnegative short integers $d(w),b(w),\lambda(w)$ are computable in polynomial time and satisfy $$\deg V_w\le d(w),\qquad \left\lVert V_w\right\rVert_1\le2^{b(w)}.$$ The zero polynomial is permitted and satisfies every nonnegative degree bound.

3.  For some fixed $a$, evaluation $$(w,p,z)\longmapsto V_w(z)\bmod p,
     \qquad z\in\mathbb F_p^{n(w)},$$ is in $\mathsf F_{a}$ on prime inputs $p>\lambda(w)$. Field elements are given by their least nonnegative binary representatives.

The integers $d,b,\lambda$ depend only on $w$, not on the evaluation prime or evaluation point.

</div>

The word $w$ may include discrete choices and short indices.
